with dyadic partitions of unity
\{\varphi^{(i)}_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
for each
i\in[d]
.
For
j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
x\in\mathbb{R}
we now define
\psi^{(i)}_{j}(x):=\varphi^{(i)}_{j}(|x|)
and put
\psi_{\bar{j}}:=\psi^{(1)}_{j_{1}}\otimes\ldots\otimes\psi^{(d)}_{j_{d}}
. Then
\{\psi_{\bar{j}}\}_{\bar{j}}\in\Phi_{\rm hyp}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and this decomposition is symmetric in the sense

\displaystyle\psi_{\bar{j}}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d})=\psi_{\bar{j}}(\pm x_{1},\ldots,\pm x_{d})\quad\text{for all}\quad(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d})\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\,.

(28)

Let

\tilde{f}:=\mathcal{P}f
be the periodization of
f\in S^{r}_{p,q}B_{\mathrm{hpc}}\left([0,1]^{d}\right)
on
\mathbb{T}^{d}\cong[-1,1]^{d}
by reflection according to Definition
2.1
.
Since
S^{r}_{p,q}B(\mathbb{T}^{d})
is independent of the utilized
decomposition of unity, up to equivalence of (quasi-)norms, we have

\displaystyle\|\tilde{f}\|_{S^{r}_{p,q}B(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\asymp\Big(\sum\limits_{\bar{j}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}}2^{|\bar{j}|_{1}rq}\|\tilde{f}_{\bar{j}}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}^{q}\Big)^{1/q}

with Littlewood-Paley blocks
\tilde{f}_{\bar{j}}
given by

\tilde{f}_{\bar{j}}:=\sum\limits_{\bar{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\psi_{j_{1}}(k_{1})\cdots\psi_{j_{d}}(k_{d})\langle\tilde{f},\overline{\exp}_{\bar{k}}\rangle_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}\exp_{\bar{k}}\,,\quad\bar{j}\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}^{d}\,.

By Lemma
2.7
, it holds
\langle\tilde{f},\overline{\exp}_{\bar{k}}\rangle_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}=2^{(d-|\bar{k}|_{0})/2}\langle f,c_{|\bar{k}|}\rangle
for every
\bar{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
.
Taking into account (
28
), we get

\tilde{f}_{\bar{j}}=\sum\limits_{\bar{k}\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}^{d}}\varphi_{j_{1}}(k_{1})\cdots\varphi_{j_{d}}(k_{d})\cdot 2^{(d-|\bar{k}|_{0})/2}\cdot\langle f,c_{\bar{k}}\rangle\cdot 2^{-(d-|\bar{k}|_{0})}\cdot\sum_{\bar{\varepsilon}\in\{-1,1\}^{d}}\exp_{(\varepsilon_{1}k_{1},\ldots,\varepsilon_{d}k_{d})}\,,

and for
x=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d})\in\mathbb{T}^{d}

\displaystyle\sum_{\bar{\varepsilon}\in\{-1,1\}^{d}}\exp_{(\varepsilon_{1}k_{1},\ldots,\varepsilon_{d}k_{d})}(x)

\displaystyle=2^{-d/2}\sum_{\bar{\varepsilon}\in\{-1,1\}^{d}}\prod_{j=1}^{d}\exp(\mathrm{i}\pi\varepsilon_{j}k_{j}x_{j})

\displaystyle=2^{-d/2}\big(\exp(\mathrm{i}\pi k_{d}x_{d})+\exp(-\mathrm{i}\pi k_{d}x_{d})\big)\sum_{\bar{\varepsilon}\in\{-1,1\}^{d-1}}\prod_{j=1}^{d-1}\exp(\mathrm{i}\pi\varepsilon_{j}k_{j}x_{j})

\displaystyle=2^{1-d/2}\cos(\pi k_{d}x_{d})\sum_{\bar{\varepsilon}\in\{-1,1\}^{d-1}}\prod_{j=1}^{d-1}\exp(\mathrm{i}\pi\varepsilon_{j}k_{j}x_{j})=\ldots=2^{d}\cos_{\bar{k}}(x)\,.

We thus arrive at

\displaystyle\tilde{f}_{\bar{j}}=2^{d/2}\sum\limits_{\bar{k}\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}^{d}}\varphi_{j_{1}}(k_{1})\cdots\varphi_{j_{d}}(k_{d})\cdot 2^{|\bar{k}|_{0}/2}\cdot\langle f,c_{\bar{k}}\rangle\cdot\cos_{\bar{k}}\,,

and further, using Lemma
2.6
,

\tilde{f}_{\bar{j}}=2^{d/2}\sum\limits_{\bar{k}\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}^{d}}\varphi_{j_{1}}(k_{1})\cdots\varphi_{j_{d}}(k_{d})\cdot 2^{|\bar{k}|_{0}/2}\cdot\langle f,c_{\bar{k}}\rangle\cdot 2^{-(|\bar{k}|_{0}+d)/2}\cdot\mathcal{P}(c_{\bar{k}})=\mathcal{P}(f^{\mathrm{hpc}}_{\bar{j}})\,.

Finally, due to the symmetry of
\tilde{f}_{\bar{j}}=\mathcal{P}(f^{\mathrm{hpc}}_{\bar{j}})
,

\displaystyle\|\tilde{f}_{\bar{j}}\|^{p}_{L_{p}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}=\|\mathcal{P}(f^{\mathrm{hpc}}_{\bar{j}})\|^{p}_{L_{p}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}=2^{d}\|\mathcal{P}(f^{\mathrm{hpc}}_{\bar{j}})\|^{p}_{L_{p}([0,1]^{d})}=2^{d}\|f^{\mathrm{hpc}}_{\bar{j}}\|^{p}_{L_{p}([0,1]^{d})}\,.

Note that the restriction operator
\mathcal{R}
is here a left-inverse of the periodization
\mathcal{P}
.
∎

Several structural properties of
S^{r}_{p,q}B_{\mathrm{hpc}}\left([0,1]^{d}\right)
can be easily deduced from the identification given in Theorem
3.13
.

•

(Quasi-)Banach space property

We know that
S^{r}_{p,q}B(\mathbb{T}^{d})_{\rm even}
, as a closed subspace of
S^{r}_{p,q}B(\mathbb{T}^{d})
, is a (quasi-)Banach space. Applying Theorem
3.13
now directly
yields the (quasi-)Banach space property of
S^{r}_{p,q}B_{\mathrm{hpc}}\left([0,1]^{d}\right)
.

•

Embeddings

Starting from an embedding
S^{r_{1}}_{p_{1},q_{1}}B(\mathbb{T}^{d})\hookrightarrow S^{r_{2}}_{p_{2},q_{2}}B(\mathbb{T}^{d})
, restriction to the even functions first yields the embedding
S^{r_{1}}_{p_{1},q_{1}}B(\mathbb{T}^{d})_{\rm even}\hookrightarrow S^{r_{2}}_{p_{2},q_{2}}B(\mathbb{T}^{d})_{\rm even}
. Theorem
3.13
then
leads to
S^{r_{1}}_{p_{1},q_{1}}B_{\mathrm{hpc}}\left([0,1]^{d}\right)\hookrightarrow S^{r_{2}}_{p_{2},q_{2}}B_{\mathrm{hpc}}\left([0,1]^{d}\right)
.

•

Independence of the partition of unity

To establish the independence of

S^{r}_{p,q}B_{\mathrm{hpc}}\left([0,1]^{d}\right)
from the utilized partition of unity, up to equivalence of
(quasi-)norms,
let us start with two different partitions
\{\varphi_{\bar{j}}\}_{\bar{j}}
and
\{\tilde{\varphi}_{\bar{j}}\}_{\bar{j}}
from
\Phi_{\rm hyp}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and let
S^{r}_{p,q}B_{\mathrm{hpc}}\left([0,1]^{d}\right)^{\varphi}
and
S^{r}_{p,q}B_{\mathrm{hpc}}\left([0,1]^{d}\right)^{\tilde{\varphi}}
denote the associated half-period cosine spaces.
As in the proof of Theorem
3.13
, we further construct
corresponding partitions
\{\psi_{\bar{j}}\}_{\bar{j}}
and
\{\tilde{\psi}_{\bar{j}}\}_{\bar{j}}
via

\psi_{\bar{j}}:=\varphi_{\bar{j}}(|\cdot|)
and
\tilde{\psi}_{\bar{j}}:=\tilde{\varphi}_{\bar{j}}(|\cdot|)
. Letting
S^{r}_{p,q}B(\mathbb{T}^{d})^{\psi}
and
S^{r}_{p,q}B(\mathbb{T}^{d})^{\tilde{\psi}}

denote the associated Besov spaces, we then have

\displaystyle S^{r}_{p,q}B_{\mathrm{hpc}}\left([0,1]^{d}\right)^{\varphi}\cong S^{r}_{p,q}B(\mathbb{T}^{d})_{\rm even}^{\psi}\cong S^{r}_{p,q}B(\mathbb{T}^{d})_{\rm even}^{\tilde{\psi}}\cong S^{r}_{p,q}B_{\mathrm{hpc}}\left([0,1]^{d}\right)^{\tilde{\varphi}}

since each partition from
\Phi_{\rm hyp}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
yields the same space
S^{r}_{p,q}B(\mathbb{T}^{d})
, up to equivalence of (quasi-)norms.

3.3
Applications of Theorem
3.13

Let us finally take the time to demonstrate the application of Theorem
3.13
in conjunction with the periodization principle established in Section
2
. For this note that, utilizing the identification
\mathbb{T}^{d}\cong[-1,1]^{d}
and the restriction operator
\mathcal{R}
from Definition
2.2
,
Theorem
3.13
can be restated in the form

\displaystyle S^{r}_{p,q}B_{\mathrm{hpc}}\left([0,1]^{d}\right)\cong\mathcal{R}\big(S^{r}_{p,q}B(\mathbb{T}^{d})_{\rm even}\big)\,.

(29)

Building on Lemma
2.10
and Lemma
2.11
, respectively, we give
two examples for the translation mechanism from
S^{r}_{p,q}B(\mathbb{T}^{d})_{\rm even}
to
S^{r}_{p,q}B_{\mathrm{hpc}}\left([0,1]^{d}\right)
.
The first example is half-period cosine approximation, where Theorem
3.14
provides an upper bound for the convergence rate of least-squares approximation from point samples, the second example is cubature.

3.3.1
Half-period cosine approximation in
S^{r}_{p,q}B_{\mathrm{hpc}}\left([0,1]^{d}\right)

In view of (
29
) and Lemma
2.10
,
a half-period cosine approximation scheme
\{A^{(\sigma)}_{{\rm hpc},N}\}_{\raisebox{-1.80835pt}{$\scriptstyle{N\in\mathbb{N}}$}}
in
S^{r}_{p,q}B_{\mathrm{hpc}}\left([0,1]^{d}\right)
performs as well as the corresponding Fourier approximation scheme
\{A^{(\sigma)}_{N}\}_{\raisebox{-1.80835pt}{$\scriptstyle{N\in\mathbb{N}}$}}
in
S^{r}_{p,q}B(\mathbb{T}^{d})_{\rm even}
(see Subsection
2.4
).
The performance of the latter in
S^{r}_{p,q}B(\mathbb{T}^{d})
thus provides an upper bound for the performance of
\{A^{(\sigma)}_{{\rm hpc},N}\}_{\raisebox{-1.80835pt}{$\scriptstyle{N\in\mathbb{N}}$}}
in
S^{r}_{p,q}B_{\mathrm{hpc}}\left([0,1]^{d}\right)
.
Fourier approximation in
S^{r}_{p,q}B(\mathbb{T}^{d})
is well-understood.
Optimal approximation schemes are obtained for the choice
\sigma(N):=\{|\bar{k}|:\bar{k}\in\Gamma_{N}\}
, where

\Gamma_{N}\subset\mathbb{Z}^{d}
are the hyperbolic cross frequencies at level
N
(see
[
17
, page 21]
),

\displaystyle\Gamma_{N}:=\Big\{\bar{k}=(k_{1},\ldots,k_{d})\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\penalty\ :\penalty\ \prod_{i=1}^{d}(1+|k_{i}|)\leq N\Big\}\quad\text{with}\quad|\Gamma_{N}|\asymp N\log(N)^{d-1}\,.

The optimal rates for orthogonal
L_{q}
approximation are given by the orthowidths (see
[
17
, page 47]
),

\displaystyle\varphi_{n}(S^{r}_{p,\theta}B(\mathbb{T}^{d}),L_{q}):=\inf\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}u_{1},\ldots,u_{n}\\
\text{orthonormal}\end{subarray}}\sup\limits_{f\in U^{r}_{p,\theta}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\Big\|f-\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}\langle f,\overline{u}_{j}\rangle_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}u_{j}\Big\|_{L_{q}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\,,

where
U^{r}_{p,\theta}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
denotes the unit ball in
S^{r}_{p,\theta}B(\mathbb{T}^{d})
.
Consequently, the rates determined in
[
17
, Sect. 4]

for these quantities
are upper bounds for the corresponding half-period cosine approximation in
S^{r}_{p,q}B_{\mathrm{hpc}}\left([0,1]^{d}\right)
.
Taking Theorem
4.2
from the next section into consideration, these upper bounds also apply to functions
f\in S^{r}_{p,q}B([0,1]^{d})
as long as
\sigma_{p}<r<\min\{1+\tfrac{1}{p},2\}
.

For
f\in H^{r}_{\rm mix}([0,1]^{d})=S^{r}_{2,2}B([0,1]^{d})
in the range
0<r<\tfrac{3}{2}
this implies that the approximation scheme
\{A^{(\sigma)}_{{\rm hpc},N}\}_{\raisebox{-1.80835pt}{$\scriptstyle{N\in\mathbb{N}}$}}
yields the optimal
L_{2}
approximation rate of
N^{-r}
, i.e.
n^{-r}(\log{n})^{r(d-1)}
expressed in the dimension
n
of the space
V_{\mathrm{hpc},N}:=\Span\{c_{|\bar{k}|}:\bar{k}\in\Gamma_{N}\}
.
In the boundary case
r=\tfrac{3}{2}
,
p=2
, one gets a rate of
n^{-3/2}(\log{n})^{2(d-1)}
for
f\in S^{3/2}_{2,1}B([0,1]^{d})
.
Indeed, if we let

\alpha(r,p,\theta)_{n}:=\varphi_{n}(S^{r}_{p,\theta}B(\mathbb{T}^{d}),L_{2})
,
the above rates follow directly from

\displaystyle\alpha(r,2,2)_{n}\asymp n^{-r}(\log n)^{r(d-1)}\quad\text{and}\quad\alpha(\tfrac{3}{2},2,\infty)_{n}\asymp n^{-3/2}(\log n)^{2(d-1)}\,,

established in
[
17
, Thm. 4.4.6 & Thm. 4.4.4]
, and the embedding
S^{3/2}_{2,1}B([0,1]^{d})\hookrightarrow S^{3/2}_{2,\infty}B_{\mathrm{hpc}}\left([0,1]^{d}\right)
in Theorem
4.4
.

3.3.2
Least-squares approximation

Using
[
38
, Prop. 1]
, we can further derive the existence of associated least-squares reconstruction schemes. To this end, we choose a sequence of subspaces
V_{n}:=\Span\{\exp(2\pi\mathrm{i}\bar{k}\cdot):|\bar{k}|\in H_{n}\}

of
L_{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
, where
\mathbb{T}^{d}\cong[0,1]^{d}
and the index sets
H_{n}\subset\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}
are chosen according to a hyperbolic cross pattern and, in addition, satisfy
|H_{n}|=n
.
We further define the operator

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}:\;L_{1}([0,1]^{d})\to L_{1}([0,1]^{d})\;,\quad f\mapsto 2^{-d}\sum\limits_{\bar{\varepsilon}\in\{0,1\}^{d}}f(\omega_{\varepsilon_{1}}(x_{1}),\ldots,\omega_{\varepsilon_{d}}(x_{d}))

with

\omega_{0}(x):=x
and
\omega_{1}(x):=1-x
for
x\in[0,1]
.
The operator
\mathcal{E}

makes any function
f\in L_{1}([0,1]^{d})
‘even’ in the sense that

(\mathcal{E}f)(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d})=(\mathcal{E}f)(\omega_{\varepsilon_{1}}(x_{1}),\ldots,\omega_{\varepsilon_{d}}(x_{d}))
for every
\bar{\varepsilon}\in\{0,1\}^{d}
. This property corresponds to
d
-fold evenness on
[-1,1]^{d}
.

For subspaces
V\subset L_{2}([0,1]^{d})
, let us denote the associated orthogonal projection by
\Pi_{V}
.
We then have, for any even
f\in L_{2}([0,1]^{d})
,

\displaystyle\|f-\Pi_{\mathcal{E}V_{n}}f\|_{L_{2}([0,1]^{d})}\leq\|f-\mathcal{E}\Pi_{V_{n}}f\|_{L_{2}([0,1]^{d})}\leq\|f-\Pi_{V_{n}}f\|_{L_{2}([0,1]^{d})}\,.

Since the image
f^{\phi}
of a function
f\in L_{1}([0,1]^{d})
under the tent transform
\phi
is even on
[0,1]^{d}
, we may define
W_{n}:=\phi^{-1}\mathcal{E}V_{n}
and thus get a sequence
\{W_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
of nested subspaces in
L_{2}([0,1]^{d})
with
\dim(W_{n})=n
. The spaces
W_{n}
have the form

W_{n}=\Span\{c_{\bar{k}}:\bar{k}\in H_{n}\}
.
Since
(\Pi_{W_{n}}f)^{\phi}=\Pi_{\mathcal{E}V_{n}}f^{\phi}
and
\|f^{\phi}\|_{L_{2}([0,1]^{d})}=\|f\|_{L_{2}([0,1]^{d})}
, it holds for any
f\in L_{2}([0,1]^{d})

\displaystyle\|f-\Pi_{W_{n}}f\|_{L_{2}([0,1]^{d})}=\|f^{\phi}-\Pi_{\mathcal{E}V_{n}}f^{\phi}\|_{L_{2}([0,1]^{d})}\leq\|f^{\phi}-\Pi_{V_{n}}f^{\phi}\|_{L_{2}([0,1]^{d})}\,.

(30)

Next, with
U^{3/2}_{2,1}([0,1]^{d})
the unit ball in
S^{3/2}_{2,1}B([0,1]^{d})
and
U^{3/2}_{2,\infty}([0,1]^{d})_{\mathrm{hpc}}
the unit ball in
S^{3/2}_{2,\infty}B_{\mathrm{hpc}}\left([0,1]^{d}\right)
, we derive with Theorem
4.4

\displaystyle\beta_{n}:=\sup_{f\in U^{3/2}_{2,1}([0,1]^{d})}\|f-\Pi_{W_{n}}f\|_{L_{2}([0,1]^{d})}\lesssim\sup_{f\in U^{3/2}_{2,\infty}([0,1]^{d})_{\mathrm{hpc}}}\|f-\Pi_{W_{n}}f\|_{L_{2}([0,1]^{d})}\,.

Let
U^{3/2}_{2,\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
denote the unit ball in
S^{3/2}_{2,1}B(\mathbb{T}^{d})
and
U^{3/2}_{2,\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d})_{\rm even}
its subset of even functions.
With (
30
), then

\displaystyle\beta_{n}\lesssim\sup_{f\in U^{3/2}_{2,\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d})_{\rm even}}\|f-\Pi_{V_{n}}f\|_{L_{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\leq\sup_{f\in U^{3/2}_{2,\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\|f-\Pi_{V_{n}}f\|_{L_{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}

and, due to the elaborations in
[
17
, Sect. 4]
and
n\leq\dim(V_{n})\leq 2^{d}n
,

\displaystyle\sup_{f\in U^{3/2}_{2,\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\|f-\Pi_{V_{n}}f\|_{L_{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\asymp\alpha(\tfrac{3}{2},2,\infty)_{\dim(V_{n})}\lesssim\alpha(\tfrac{3}{2},2,\infty)_{n}\,.

Now we can apply
[
38
, Prop. 1]
. This yields the following theorem.

Theorem 3.14
.

There is an absolute constant
c>0
such that for all
m\in\mathbb{N}

there are point sets
\{x_{j}\}_{j=1}^{n}\subset[0,1]^{d}
and functions
\{\varphi_{j}\}_{j=1}^{n}\subset W_{n}
with
n\leq cm
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{f\in U^{3/2}_{2,1}([0,1]^{d})}\|f-A_{m}(f)\|_{L_{2}([0,1]^{d})}\leq\frac{70}{\sqrt{m}}\sum_{k>\lfloor m/4\rfloor}\frac{\beta_{k}}{\sqrt{k}}\lesssim m^{-3/2}(\log m)^{2(d-1)}\,,

where
A_{m}(f):=\sum_{i=1}^{n}f(x_{i})\varphi_{i}
is a weighted least-squares reconstruction algorithm in the space
W_{n}

Note that this theorem in particular applies to functions
f\in H^{r}_{\rm mix}([0,1]^{d})
with
r>\tfrac{3}{2}
due to the embedding
H^{3/2+\varepsilon}_{\rm mix}([0,1]^{d})\hookrightarrow S^{3/2}_{2,1}B([0,1]^{d})
for any
\varepsilon>0
. For more details on the algorithm we refer to
[
38
, Rem. 7]
.

3.3.3
Tent-transformed cubature

Turning to tent-transformed cubature, we learn
from Lemma
2.11
and Theorem
3.13

that tent-transformed rules work as well on
S^{r}_{p,q}B_{\mathrm{hpc}}\left([0,1]^{d}\right)
as the untransformed rules on
S^{r}_{p,q}B(\mathbb{T}^{d})_{\rm even}
. An upper performance bound is thus provided by
the convergence rate of the untransformed rules on
S^{r}_{p,q}B(\mathbb{T}^{d})
.
We use this insight to translate
[
13
, Thm. 3.1]
and
[
26
, Thm. 5.3]
on
cubature in
S^{r}_{p,q}B(\mathbb{T}^{d})
to tent-transformed cubature in
S^{r}_{p,q}B_{\mathrm{hpc}}\left([0,1]^{d}\right)
.
The first result
[
13
, Thm. 3.1]
deals with Fibonacci cubature in
S^{r}_{p,q}B(\mathbb{T}^{2})
.
Recall that a Fibonacci rule

\Phi_{n}(f):=\frac{1}{b_{n}}\sum_{j=1}^{b_{n}}f(x_{j})
uses the
n
th Fibonacci lattice
F_{n}:=\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{b_{n}}\}
as node set in
\mathbb{T}^{2}
, where
b_{n}
is the
n
th Fibonacci number.
According to
[
13
, Thm. 3.1]
, these rules provide the optimal rate
b_{n}^{-r}(\log b_{n})^{(1-1/q)_{+}}
in the periodic space
S^{r}_{p,q}B(\mathbb{T}^{2})
if
1\leq p\leq\infty
,
0<q\leq\infty
, and
r>\tfrac{1}{p}
.
The associated tent-transformed rules thus provide at least the same rate
in
S^{r}_{p,q}B_{\mathrm{hpc}}\left([0,1]^{2}\right)
, and due to Theorem
4.2
and the embedding
S^{r}_{p,q}B([0,1]^{2})\hookrightarrow S^{r}_{1,q}B([0,1]^{2})
from Proposition
A.5

these rates also hold in
S^{r}_{p,q}B([0,1]^{2})
in the range

1\leq p\leq\infty
,
0<q\leq\infty
, and
\tfrac{1}{p}<r<2
.
To deduce a similar result for
d>2
, we resort to digital nets since for general parameter constellations optimal lattice constructions are not known for the periodic spaces
S^{r}_{p,q}B(\mathbb{T}^{d})
.
As shown in
[
26
, Thm. 5.3]
, restricting to
1\leq p,q\leq\infty
and
\tfrac{1}{p}<r<2
, nets of order
2
exist with the optimal rate
n^{-r}(\log n)^{(d-1)(1-1/q)}
in
S^{r}_{p,q}B(\mathbb{T}^{d})
,
confer also
[
26
, Rem. 5.4, Thm. 5.5]
.
Applying the translation mechanism as before, we now get
at least these rates for the tent-transformed digital nets
in
S^{r}_{p,q}B_{\mathrm{hpc}}\left([0,1]^{d}\right)
as well as in
S^{r}_{p,q}B([0,1]^{d})
.
Let us finally consider the Hilbert space
H^{2}_{\rm mix}([0,1]^{d})
. For arbitrarily small
\varepsilon>0
, by Theorem
4.2
and Proposition
A.5
, there holds

H^{2}_{\rm mix}([0,1]^{d})=S^{2}_{2,2}B([0,1]^{d})\hookrightarrow S^{2-\varepsilon}_{1,1}B([0,1]^{d})\cong S^{2-\varepsilon}_{1,1}B_{\mathrm{hpc}}\left([0,1]^{d}\right)\,.

The tent-transformed digital nets from above, resp. the Fibonacci rules if
d=2
, thus provide at least
n^{\varepsilon-2}
as convergence rate. This
fits with numerical observations and other theoretical results on tent-transformed cubature in
H^{2}_{\rm mix}([0,1]^{d})
. Hickernell, in 2002, was the first to obtain this rate using randomly shifted lattices
[
25
]
. A similar result for randomly shifted digital nets
[
12
]
was proved in 2007 by Cristea, Dick, Leobacher, and Pillichshammer.
A notable recent result from 2019 is due to
Goda, Suzuki, and Yoshiki
[
21
, Thm. 2, Cor. 1]
, who could also confirm it for tent-transformed lattice rules without shifting for all
d\in\mathbb{N}
, using a component-by-component construction. Due to the unknown performance of such lattices in
S^{2-\varepsilon}_{1,q}B(\mathbb{T}^{d})
,
our analysis cannot to date reproduce this finding.
But it complements the picture.
The mentioned former results were obtained by Hilbert space methodology.
Our analysis, in contrast, is based on embeddings into the half-period cosine scale
S^{r}_{p,q}B_{\mathrm{hpc}}\left([0,1]^{d}\right)
. Hereby,
the necessity to include non-Hilbert spaces becomes apparent as e.g.
H^{r}_{\rm mix}([0,1]^{d})
with
\tfrac{3}{2}<r<2
does not embed into
S^{r}_{2,2}B_{\mathrm{hpc}}\left([0,1]^{d}\right)
but into the slightly larger space
S^{r}_{1,2}B_{\mathrm{hpc}}\left([0,1]^{d}\right)
, which gives the rate
n^{-r}(\log n)^{(d-1)/2}
.

4
Relation of
S^{r}_{p,q}B_{\mathrm{hpc}}\left([0,1]^{d}\right)
to
S^{r}_{p,q}B([0,1]^{d})

We next analyze the relation of
S^{r}_{p,q}B_{\mathrm{hpc}}\left([0,1]^{d}\right)

to the classical non-periodic Besov space

S^{r}_{p,q}B([0,1]^{d})
, the latter defined as restriction of the respective space on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
(see Definition
3.6
). Recall that our analysis is limited to the case
r>\sigma_{p}
.
Theorem
3.13
and Proposition
3.10
directly lead to the following theorem.

Theorem 4.1
.

For parameters in the range
0<p,q\leq\infty
,
\sigma_{p}<r
, we have the embedding

\displaystyle\operatorname{Id}:\;S^{r}_{p,q}B_{\mathrm{hpc}}\left([0,1]^{d}\right)\hookrightarrow S^{r}_{p,q}B([0,1]^{d})\,.

Proof.

This is a consequence of
\operatorname{Id}=\mathcal{R}\circ\mathcal{P}
, Theorem
3.13
, and Proposition
3.10
.
∎

The space
S^{r}_{p,q}B_{\mathrm{hpc}}\left([0,1]^{d}\right)
is hence contained in the space

S^{r}_{p,q}B([0,1]^{d})
. As the identification in Theorem
3.13
shows,
functions from
S^{r}_{p,q}B_{\mathrm{hpc}}\left([0,1]^{d}\right)
feature additional regularity conditions at the boundary of
[0,1]^{d}
. This makes these spaces potentially smaller than
S^{r}_{p,q}B([0,1]^{d})
. For low regularity, however, they are in fact equal.

Theorem 4.2
.

In case
0<p,q\leq\infty
and
\sigma_{p}<r<\min\{1+\tfrac{1}{p},2\}

\displaystyle\operatorname{Id}:\;S^{r}_{p,q}B_{\mathrm{hpc}}\left([0,1]^{d}\right)\cong S^{r}_{p,q}B([0,1]^{d})\,.

Proof.

Assuming that the periodization operator
\mathcal{P}
from Definition
2.1
is a bounded operator from

S^{r}_{p,q}B(\mathbb{R}^{d})
to
S^{r}_{p,q}B(\mathbb{T}^{d})
, in which case it is also a bounded operator from

S^{r}_{p,q}B([0,1]^{d})
to
S^{r}_{p,q}B(\mathbb{T}^{d})
, we can argue as follows. The image
of
S^{r}_{p,q}B([0,1]^{d})
under
\mathcal{P}
is
S^{r}_{p,q}B(\mathbb{T}^{d})_{\rm even}
. Further, by the periodization principle, Theorem
3.13
,
S^{r}_{p,q}B(\mathbb{T}^{d})_{\rm even}
is mapped isomorphically to
S^{r}_{p,q}B_{\mathrm{hpc}}\left([0,1]^{d}\right)
by the restriction operator
\mathcal{R}
from Definition
2.2
. Since
\operatorname{Id}=\mathcal{R}\circ\mathcal{P}
,
we obtain the result. The proof can be pictured nicely in a commutative diagram:
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{r}_{p,q}B([0,1]^{d})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{r}_{p,q}B(\mathbb{T}^{d})_{\rm even}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{r}_{p,q}B_{\mathrm{hpc}}\left([0,1]^{d}\right)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Id}=\mathcal{R}\circ\mathcal{P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{R}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

Note that the down-arrow on the right depicts the isomorphism according to the periodization principle. It remains to determine the range of
r
, where
\mathcal{P}:S^{r}_{p,q}B(\mathbb{R}^{d})\to S^{r}_{p,q}B(\mathbb{T}^{d})
is bounded. This question is answered in Theorem
4.3
.
∎

The following theorem closes the gap
in the proof of Theorem
4.2
.

Theorem 4.3
.

Let
0<p,q\leq\infty
and
\sigma_{p}<r<\min\{1+\tfrac{1}{p},2\}
. Then

\mathcal{P}:\;S^{r}_{p,q}B(\mathbb{R}^{d})\to S^{r}_{p,q}B(\mathbb{T}^{d})

is a bounded linear operator. Further,
\mathcal{P}
is bounded as operator

\mathcal{P}:\;S^{r}_{p,\min\{p,1\}}B(\mathbb{R}^{d})\to S^{r}_{p,\infty}B(\mathbb{T}^{d})

for
r=\min\{1+\tfrac{1}{p},2\}
and either
\tfrac{1}{3}<p<1
or
1<p\leq\infty
.

A consequence of the second statement in Theorem
4.3
is an interesting endpoint result.

Theorem 4.4
.

Let
r=\min\{1+\tfrac{1}{p},2\}
. In case
\tfrac{1}{3}<p<1
or
1<p\leq\infty
we have the embedding

\displaystyle\operatorname{Id}:\;S^{r}_{p,\min\{p,1\}}B([0,1]^{d})\hookrightarrow S^{r}_{p,\infty}B_{\mathrm{hpc}}\left([0,1]^{d}\right)\,.

Proof.

The proof
is analogous to the proof of Theorem
4.2
.
∎

The remainder of this section is devoted to the proof of Theorem
4.3
. It is
presented in Subsection
4.3
.
Before, let us recall some tools in the following two subsections, beginning with the Chui-Wang wavelets of
2
nd order.

4.1
Chui-Wang wavelets of order
2

In 1992, Charles Chui and Jian-Zhong Wang
[
10
]

introduced a class of compactly supported spline wavelets generalizing the well-known Haar wavelet to higher orders
m\in\mathbb{N}
. They constructed bases of such wavelets in
L_{2}(\mathbb{R})
, nowadays called Chui-Wang wavelets, for every order
m
.
These bases consist of
m
th order spline functions, i.e. functions that are piecewise assembled from polynomials of degree
m-1
. For
m=1
they replicate the Haar basis, which is orthonormal. In case
m\geq 2
the bases are not orthonormal any more, yet, they still represent well-behaved Riesz bases with nice duality properties. They are for instance semi-orthogonal and the dual bases can be calculated explicitly (see e.g.
[
9
,
10
]
and
[
15
, Sec. 2.2]
).

In our proof of Theorem
4.3
we will make use of multivariate Chui-Wang bases of order
2
. Their relevant features for us are their compact support, two vanishing moments and their piecewise linear structure.
They are constructed from the original univariate bases via tensorization.
Let us therefore recall the original construction in
L_{2}(\mathbb{R})
first.
Following
[
10
, Thm. 1]
, the
2
nd order univariate Chui-Wang wavelet
\psi_{2}
can be written in the form (see formula (2.3) in
[
10
]
)

\psi_{2}(x)=\sum_{l=0}^{4}q_{l}N_{2}(2x-l)\quad\text{with}\quad q_{l}=\frac{(-1)^{l}}{2}\sum_{i=0}^{2}\binom{2}{i}N_{4}(l-i+1)\,,

where
N_{2}
stands for the
2
nd order cardinal
B
-spline
N_{2}:=\chi_{[0,1)}\ast\chi_{[0,1)}
and
N_{4}:=N_{2}\ast N_{2}
for the
4
th order cardinal
B
-spline.
For
l\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
k\in\mathbb{Z}
, one then sets

\psi_{-1,k}(x):=N_{2}(x-k)\quad\text{and}\quad\psi_{l,k}(t):=\psi_{2}(2^{l}x-k)\,.

(31)

The collection
\{\psi_{l,k}:l\in\mathbb{N}_{-1},k\in\mathbb{Z}\}
is called the univariate Chui-Wang wavelet system of order
2
.
The generators
\psi_{0,0}=\psi_{2}
and
\psi_{-1,0}=N_{2}
are sometimes referred to as the
2
nd order mother and father Chui-Wang wavelet. They have the
explicit form

\displaystyle\psi_{0,0}(x)=\begin{cases}\tfrac{1}{6}x&,\,0\leq x<\tfrac{1}{2}\,,\\
-\tfrac{7}{6}x+\tfrac{2}{3}&,\,\tfrac{1}{2}\leq x<1\,,\\
\tfrac{8}{3}x-\tfrac{19}{6}&,\,1\leq x<\tfrac{3}{2}\,,\\
-\tfrac{8}{3}x+\tfrac{29}{6}&,\,\tfrac{3}{2}\leq x<2\,,\\
\tfrac{7}{6}x-\tfrac{17}{6}&,\,2\leq x<\tfrac{5}{2}\,,\\
-\tfrac{1}{6}x+\tfrac{1}{2}&,\,\tfrac{5}{2}\leq x<3\,,\\
0&,\,\text{otherwise}\,.\end{cases}\quad,\quad\psi_{-1,0}(x)=\begin{cases}x&,\,0\leq x<1\,,\\
2-x&,\,1\leq x<2\,,\\
0&,\,\text{otherwise}\,.\end{cases}

(32)

Hence, with certain coefficients
c^{(0)}_{\kappa}\in\mathbb{R}
and
c^{(-1)}_{\kappa}\in\mathbb{R}
, it holds

\displaystyle\psi_{0,0}(x)=\sum_{\kappa=0}^{6}c^{(0)}_{\kappa}\big(x-\tfrac{\kappa}{2}\big)_{+}\quad\text{and}\quad\psi_{-1,0}(x)=\sum_{\kappa=0}^{6}c^{(-1)}_{\kappa}\big(x-\tfrac{\kappa}{2}\big)_{+}\,.

(33)

Note that, due to the half-integer shifts, we have here

c^{(-1)}_{\kappa}=0
for
\kappa\in\{1,3,5,6\}
.

The dual Chui-Wang basis
\{\tilde{\psi}_{l,k}:l\in\mathbb{N}_{-1},k\in\mathbb{Z}\}
of order
2
is generated as in (
31
) from modified generators

\displaystyle\tilde{\psi}_{0,0}:=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}a^{(0)}_{n}\psi_{0,n}\quad\text{and}\quad\tilde{\psi}_{-1,0}:=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}a^{(-1)}_{n}\psi_{-1,n}\,,

(34)

where
\{a^{(0)}_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}
and
\{a^{(-1)}_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}
are some specific sequences in
\mathbb{C}
(see
[
15
, Sec. 2.2]
for an explicit calculation).
These sequences feature exponential decay, which means there exists
c>1
so that

|a^{(\varepsilon)}_{n}|\lesssim c^{-|n|}\quad\text{for $n\in\mathbb{Z}$,\, $\varepsilon\in\{-1,0\}$}\,.

The relation to the primal Chui-Wang basis is expressed by the biorthogonality relation (see
[
10
, Thm. 3]
)

\displaystyle\langle\psi_{j,k},\tilde{\psi}_{l,m}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}}=2^{-(j_{+}+l_{+})/2}\delta_{j,l}\delta_{k,m}\,.

(35)

Finally, to arrive at the desired multivariate generalization, we take tensor products,
i.e. for
x=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d})\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
we let

\displaystyle\psi_{\bar{l},\bar{k}}(x):=\psi_{l_{1},k_{1}}(x_{1})\cdots\psi_{l_{d},k_{d}}(x_{d})\quad\text{and}\quad\tilde{\psi}_{\bar{l},\bar{k}}(x):=\tilde{\psi}_{l_{1},k_{1}}(x_{1})\cdots\tilde{\psi}_{l_{d},k_{d}}(x_{d})\,.

(36)

The primal and dual tensor Chui-Wang wavelet systems on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
then look like

\displaystyle\{\psi_{\bar{l},\bar{k}}:(\bar{l},\bar{k})\in\Gamma^{d}\}\quad\text{and}\quad\{\tilde{\psi}_{\bar{l},\bar{k}}:(\bar{l},\bar{k})\in\Gamma^{d}\}\quad\text{with index set }\Gamma^{d}:=\mathbb{N}_{-1}^{d}\times\mathbb{Z}^{d}\,.

From (
35
) we obtain

\displaystyle\langle\psi_{\bar{j},\bar{k}},\tilde{\psi}_{\bar{l},\bar{m}}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}=2^{-(\bar{j}_{+}+\bar{l}_{+})/2}\delta_{\bar{j},\bar{l}}\delta_{\bar{k},\bar{m}}\,.

(37)

These systems thus
constitute a pair of dual Riesz bases and
every
f\in L_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
can be expanded in the form

f=\sum_{(\bar{j},\bar{k})\in\Gamma^{d}}2^{\bar{j}_{+}}\langle f,\tilde{\psi}_{\bar{j},\bar{k}}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\psi_{\bar{j},\bar{k}}=\sum_{(\bar{j},\bar{k})\in\Gamma^{d}}2^{\bar{j}_{+}}\langle f,\psi_{\bar{j},\bar{k}}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\tilde{\psi}_{\bar{j},\bar{k}}

with unconditional convergence in
L_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. The factor
2^{\bar{j}_{+}}
is due to the
biorthogonality relation (
37
).

We further have

\displaystyle\|f\|_{L_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\|\{2^{\bar{j}_{+}/2}\langle f,\psi_{\bar{j},\bar{k}}\rangle\}_{\bar{j},\bar{k}}\|_{\ell_{2}(\Gamma^{d})}\asymp\|\{2^{\bar{j}_{+}/2}\langle f,\tilde{\psi}_{\bar{j},\bar{k}}\rangle\}_{\bar{j},\bar{k}}\|_{\ell_{2}(\Gamma^{d})}\,.

In Section
5
we will investigate the generalization of these basis properties to the space
S^{r}_{p,q}B(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. The result is formulated in Theorem
5.1
.

Concluding this subsection, let us note that we can derive from (
34
) the representation

\displaystyle\tilde{\psi}_{\bar{l},\bar{k}}=\sum_{\bar{n}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}a^{(\bar{l})}_{\bar{n}}\psi_{\bar{l},\bar{k}+\bar{n}}\quad\text{with}\quad a^{(\bar{l})}_{\bar{n}}:=\prod_{i\in[d]}a^{(l_{i}^{-})}_{n_{i}}

(38)

of the dual wavelets for any
(\bar{l},\bar{k})\in\Gamma^{d}
, where
l_{i}^{-}=\min\{0,l_{i}\}
. For the coefficients’ decay we get

\displaystyle|a^{(\bar{l})}_{\bar{n}}|\lesssim c^{-|\bar{n}|}\quad\text{for $\bar{n}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}$,\, $\bar{l}\in\mathbb{N}_{-1}^{d}$}\,.

(39)

4.2
Further preparation

The Lipschitz constant
L(f)
of a univariate function
f:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{C}
is defined by

\displaystyle L(f):=\sup\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}x,y\in\mathbb{R}\\
x\neq y\end{subarray}}\frac{|f(x)-f(y)|}{|x-y|}\,.

Further, the
univariate forward difference operator on
\mathbb{R}
, denoted by
\Delta_{h}
for
h\in\mathbb{R}
, is given by

\displaystyle\Delta_{h}(f,x):=f(x+h)-f(x)\;,\quad x\in\mathbb{R}\,.

Its multivariate version
\Delta_{h,i}
acting on the
i
th coordinate of a function
f:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{C}
, for
i\in[d]
, is set to be

\Delta_{h,i}(f,x)=f(x_{1},\ldots,x_{i}+h,\ldots x_{d})-f(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d})\;,\quad x=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d})\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\,.

Recursively, we then define the
m
th order difference operator
\Delta^{m}_{h,i}

for
m\in\mathbb{N}
by setting
\Delta^{m}_{h,i}:=\Delta_{h,i}\circ\Delta^{m-1}_{h,i}

and
\Delta^{0}_{h,i}:=\operatorname{Id}
. This operator acts on the
i
th coordinate as

\Delta^{m}_{h,i}(f,x)=\sum\limits_{j=0}^{m}(-1)^{j}\binom{m}{j}f(x_{1},\ldots,x_{i-1},x_{i}+(m-j)h,x_{i+1},\ldots,x_{d})\,.

Utilizing this operator, we finally introduce the multivariate difference operator
\Delta^{m,e}_{\bar{h}}
of order
m
for a subset
e\subset[d]
of indices and
\bar{h}=(h_{1},\ldots,h_{d})\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. If
e=\emptyset
we set
\Delta^{m,\emptyset}_{\bar{h}}:=\operatorname{Id}
and otherwise

\displaystyle\Delta^{m,e}_{\bar{h}}:=\prod_{i\in e}\Delta^{m}_{h_{i},i}\,.

(40)

This operator plays a crucial role in our proof of Theorem
4.3
since we rely on a characterization of
S^{r}_{p,q}B(\mathbb{R}^{d})
by rectangular means of differences.
A few other auxiliary results needed in the proof are listed below.

•

For a univariate function
f:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{C}
and for
\kappa>0

\displaystyle|[\Delta_{h}^{2}f(\kappa\cdot)](x)|\leq 2L(f)\kappa|h|\quad\text{and}\quad|[\Delta_{h}^{2}f(\kappa\cdot)](x)|\leq 4\|f\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\,.

(41)

•

It holds
L(f\circ g)\leq L(f)L(g)
for any two univariate functions
f:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{C}
and
g:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
. In particular

\displaystyle L(f\circ\rho_{1})\leq L(f)

(42)

if
\rho_{1}
is the function from (
12
) since
L(\rho_{1})=1
.

After this preparation, we are now ready for the proof of Theorem
4.3
.

4.3
Proof of Theorem
4.3

Let
\mathcal{P}
be the periodization operator from Definition
2.1
and
f\in S^{r}_{p,q}B(\mathbb{R}^{d})
with parameters
0<p,q\leq\infty
and
\sigma_{p}<r\leq 2
, where
\sigma_{p}=\max\{0,\tfrac{1}{p}-1\}
.
In this parameter range the (quasi-)norm

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{P}(f)\|_{S^{r}_{p,q}B(\mathbb{T}^{d})}=\|f\circ\rho|_{[-1,1]^{d}}\|_{S^{r}_{p,q}B(\mathbb{T}^{d})}

(43)

can be characterized
via rectangular means of differences of
3
rd order. Here we follow
[
46
, Sec. 3.3]
(cf. also
[
60
, Sec. 4.5]
). For a function
f:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{C}
, a given multiscale
\bar{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+}
, and a subset of coordinate directions
e\subset[d]
, the order
m
means of differences are defined locally at position
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
by

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{e}_{m}(f,\bar{t},x):=\int_{[-1,1]^{d}}\big|\Delta^{m,e}_{(h_{1}t_{1},\ldots,h_{d}t_{d})}(f,x)\big|\,\mathrm{d}\bar{h}=\int_{[-1,1]^{d}}\big|\Big(\prod_{i\in e}\Delta^{m}_{h_{i}t_{i},i}\Big)(f,x)\big|\,\mathrm{d}\bar{h}\,,

where
\Delta^{m,e}_{\bar{h}}
is the operator (
40
).
According to
[
46
, Thm. 3.6]
(cf. also
[
60
, Thm. 4.5.1]
for a continuous version), when we let
e(\bar{j}):=\{i:j_{i}\neq 0\}
for
\bar{j}\in\mathbb{N}^{d}_{0}
,
we have the characterization

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{P}(f)\|_{S^{r}_{p,q}B(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\asymp\Big(\sum_{\bar{j}\in\mathbb{N}^{d}_{0}}2^{|\bar{j}|_{1}rq}\Big\|\mathcal{R}^{e(\bar{j})}_{3}(f\circ\rho,2^{-\bar{j}},\cdot)\Big\|_{L_{p}([-1,1]^{d})}^{q}\Big)^{1/q}

of the (quasi-)norm (
43
). Note that
f\circ\rho
is a function on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
that is periodic with respect to the domain
[-1,1]^{d}
.
Noting
\mathcal{R}^{e}_{3}(f,\bar{t},x)\lesssim\mathcal{R}^{e}_{2}(f,\bar{t},x)
, we deduce
the following estimate

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{P}(f)\|_{S^{r}_{p,q}B(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\lesssim\Big(\sum_{\bar{j}\in\mathbb{N}^{d}_{0}}2^{|\bar{j}|_{1}rq}\Big\|\mathcal{R}^{e(\bar{j})}_{2}(f\circ\rho,2^{-\bar{j}},\cdot)\Big\|_{L_{p}([-1,1]^{d})}^{q}\Big)^{1/q}\,.

This is our starting point for proving Theorem
4.3
.
For the first statement, the estimate

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{P}(f)\|_{S^{r}_{p,q}B(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\lesssim\|f\|_{S^{r}_{p,q}B(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

(44)

is what we aim for. Towards this goal, we decompose
f
into different scales

\displaystyle f=\sum_{\bar{l}\in\mathbb{N}_{-1}^{d}}f_{\bar{l}}

(45)

of level-wise
2
nd order Chui-Wang expansions (see Theorem
5.1
for convergence properties)

\displaystyle f_{\bar{l}}:=\sum_{\bar{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}2^{\bar{l}_{+}}\langle f,\tilde{\psi}_{\bar{l},\bar{k}}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\psi_{\bar{l},\bar{k}}\,.

Recall the tensor structure
\psi_{\bar{l},\bar{k}}(x)=\psi_{l_{1},k_{1}}(x_{1})\cdots\psi_{l_{d},k_{d}}(x_{d})
and
\tilde{\psi}_{\bar{l},\bar{k}}(x)=\tilde{\psi}_{l_{1},k_{1}}(x_{1})\cdots\tilde{\psi}_{l_{d},k_{d}}(x_{d})
of the Chui-Wang wavelets in (
36
). Since we want to sum over all
\bar{l}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
in (
45
), let us put
\psi_{l_{i},k_{i}}(x_{i}):=\tilde{\psi}_{l_{i},k_{i}}(x_{i}):=0

if
l_{i}<-1
. Then
\psi_{\bar{l},\bar{k}}=\tilde{\psi}_{\bar{l},\bar{k}}\equiv 0
and
f_{\bar{l}}\equiv 0
in case
\min_{i\in[d]}\{l_{i}\}<-1
.

Using decomposition (
45
), we estimate with
u:=\min\{p,q,1\}
and
\tilde{p}:=p/u
,
\tilde{q}:=q/u
,
\tilde{r}:=ru
,

\displaystyle\Big[\eqref{fff1}\Big]^{u}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big(\sum_{\bar{j}\in\mathbb{N}^{d}_{0}}2^{|\bar{j}|_{1}rq}\Big\|\sum_{\bar{l}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\mathcal{R}^{e(\bar{j})}_{2}(f_{\bar{j}+\bar{l}}\circ\rho,2^{-\bar{j}},\cdot)\Big\|^{q}_{L_{p}([-1,1]^{d})}\Big)^{1/\tilde{q}}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\sum_{\bar{j}\in\mathbb{N}^{d}_{0}}2^{|\bar{j}|_{1}\tilde{r}\tilde{q}}\Big\|\sum_{\bar{l}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\mathcal{R}^{e(\bar{j})}_{2}(f_{\bar{j}+\bar{l}}\circ\rho,2^{-\bar{j}},\cdot)|^{u}\Big\|_{L_{\tilde{p}}([-1,1]^{d})}^{\tilde{q}}\Big)^{1/\tilde{q}}\,.

(46)

Since
\tilde{p},\tilde{q}\geq 1
, we may continue with Minkowski’s inequality and arrive at

\displaystyle\Big[\eqref{fff1}\Big]^{u}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\bar{l}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\Big(\sum_{\bar{j}\in\mathbb{N}^{d}_{0}}2^{|\bar{j}|_{1}\tilde{r}\tilde{q}}\Big\||\mathcal{R}^{e(\bar{j})}_{2}(f_{\bar{j}+\bar{l}}\circ\rho,2^{-\bar{j}},\cdot)|^{u}\Big\|_{L_{\tilde{p}}([-1,1]^{d})}^{\tilde{q}}\Big)^{1/\tilde{q}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\bar{l}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\Big(\sum_{\bar{j}\in\mathbb{N}^{d}_{0}}2^{|\bar{j}|_{1}rq}\Big\|\mathcal{R}^{e(\bar{j})}_{2}(f_{\bar{j}+\bar{l}}\circ\rho,2^{-\bar{j}},\cdot)\Big\|_{L_{p}([-1,1]^{d})}^{q}\Big)^{1/\tilde{q}}\,.

(47)

Putting
\lambda_{\bar{l},\bar{k}}:=2^{\bar{l}_{+}}\langle f,\tilde{\psi}_{\bar{l},\bar{k}}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}
for
\bar{l}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
,
where
\lambda_{\bar{l},\bar{k}}=0
if
\min_{i\in[d]}\{l_{i}\}<-1
,
we can further estimate

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{e(\bar{j})}_{2}(f_{\bar{j}+\bar{l}}\circ\rho,2^{-\bar{j}},\cdot)\lesssim\sum_{\bar{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\lambda_{\bar{j}+\bar{l},\bar{k}}|\mathcal{R}^{e(\bar{j})}_{2}(\psi_{\bar{j}+\bar{l},\bar{k}}\circ\rho,2^{-\bar{j}},\cdot)

(48)

and, due to the tensor structure of
\psi_{\bar{l},\bar{k}}
, it holds

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{e(\bar{j})}_{2}

\displaystyle(\psi_{\bar{j}+\bar{l},\bar{k}}\circ\rho,2^{-\bar{j}},x)=\int_{[-1,1]^{d}}\big|\Delta^{2,e(\bar{j})}_{(h_{1}2^{-j_{1}},\ldots,h_{d}2^{-j_{d}})}(\psi_{\bar{j}+\bar{l},\bar{k}}\circ\rho,x)\big|\,\mathrm{d}\bar{h}

\displaystyle=\prod_{i\in e(\bar{j})}\underbrace{\int\limits_{-1}^{1}\big|\Delta^{2}_{h_{i}2^{-j_{i}}}(\psi_{j_{i}+l_{i},k_{i}}\circ\rho_{1},x_{i})\big|\,\mathrm{d}h_{i}}_{F(x_{i})}\cdot\prod_{i\in e_{0}(\bar{j})}\underbrace{\int\limits_{-1}^{1}\big|\psi_{l_{i},k_{i}}\circ\rho_{1}(x_{i})\big|\,\mathrm{d}h_{i}}_{G(x_{i})}\,,

where
e_{0}(\bar{j}):=[d]\backslash e(\bar{j})
and
\rho_{1}
is the function (
12
).
The functions
F,\,G:\mathbb{R}\to[0,\infty)

are univariate and
2
-periodic.
Let us put

\displaystyle\text{I}_{j_{i},k_{i},l_{i}}(x_{i}):=\begin{cases}F(x_{i})\quad&,\quad j_{i}>0\,,\\
G(x_{i})&,\quad j_{i}=0\,.\end{cases}

We can then write conveniently

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{e(\bar{j})}_{2}(\psi_{\bar{j}+\bar{l},\bar{k}}\circ\rho,2^{-\bar{j}},x)=\prod_{i\in[d]}\text{I}_{j_{i},k_{i},l_{i}}(x_{i})\,.

We next split

\displaystyle\text{I}_{j_{i},k_{i},l_{i}}(\cdot)=\text{I}^{[0]}_{j_{i},k_{i},l_{i}}(\cdot)+\text{I}^{[1]}_{j_{i},k_{i},l_{i}}(\cdot)

with the summands

\displaystyle\text{I}^{[0]}_{j_{i},k_{i},l_{i}}:=\text{I}_{j_{i},k_{i},l_{i}}\cdot\chi_{S_{j_{i},k_{i},l_{i}}}\quad\text{and}\quad\text{I}^{[1]}_{j_{i},k_{i},l_{i}}:=\text{I}_{j_{i},k_{i},l_{i}}-\text{I}^{[0]}_{j_{i},k_{i},l_{i}}\,,

(49)

where
\chi_{S_{j_{i},k_{i},l_{i}}}
is the characteristic function of

\displaystyle S_{j_{i},k_{i},l_{i}}:=\operatorname{supp}(\psi_{j_{i}+l_{i},k_{i}}\circ\rho_{1})\,.

Then we have for
x=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d})\in\mathbb{R}^{d}

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{e(\bar{j})}_{2}(\psi_{\bar{j}+\bar{l},\bar{k}}\circ\rho,2^{-\bar{j}},x)=\prod_{i\in[d]}\Big(\text{I}^{[0]}_{j_{i},k_{i},l_{i}}(x_{i})+\text{I}^{[1]}_{j_{i},k_{i},l_{i}}(x_{i})\Big)=\sum\limits_{\bar{\varepsilon}\in\{0,1\}^{d}}\text{I}^{[\bar{\varepsilon}]}_{\bar{j},\bar{k},\bar{l}}(x)

(50)

with functions

\displaystyle\text{I}^{[\bar{\varepsilon}]}_{\bar{j},\bar{k},\bar{l}}(x):=\prod_{i\in[d]}\text{I}^{[\varepsilon_{i}]}_{j_{i},k_{i},l_{i}}(x_{i})\quad\text{for}\quad\bar{\varepsilon}=(\varepsilon_{1},\ldots,\varepsilon_{d})\in\{0,1\}^{d}\,.

From (
48
) and (
50
) we get for fixed
\bar{j}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}
and
\bar{l}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
:

\displaystyle\Big\|\mathcal{R}^{e(\bar{j})}_{2}(f_{\bar{j}+\bar{l}}\circ\rho,2^{-\bar{j}},\cdot)\Big\|_{L_{p}([-1,1]^{d})}\lesssim\sum\limits_{\bar{\varepsilon}\in\{0,1\}^{d}}\Big\|\sum_{\bar{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\lambda_{\bar{j}+\bar{l},\bar{k}}|\text{I}^{[\bar{\varepsilon}]}_{\bar{j},\bar{k},\bar{l}}(\cdot)\Big\|_{L_{p}([-1,1]^{d})}\,.

Let us next analyze some properties of the functions
\text{I}^{[\varepsilon_{i}]}_{j_{i},k_{i},l_{i}}
, which are piecewise linear and
2
-periodic. The periodicity allows to restrict the investigation to the period interval
[-1,1]
.

•

We first prove the support properties

\displaystyle\big|\operatorname{supp}(\text{I}^{[0]}_{j_{i},k_{i},l_{i}})\cap[-1,1]\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-j_{i}-l_{i}^{+}}\,,

(51)

\displaystyle\big|\operatorname{supp}(\text{I}^{[1]}_{j_{i},k_{i},l_{i}})\cap[-1,1]\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-j_{i}}\,.

(52)

Proof of (
51
) and (
52
).

To verify (
51
), we use that, by definition, the support of
\text{I}^{[0]}_{j_{i},k_{i},l_{i}}
satisfies

\displaystyle\big|\operatorname{supp}(\text{I}^{[0]}_{j_{i},k_{i},l_{i}})\cap[-1,1]\big|\leq\big|S_{j_{i},k_{i},l_{i}}\cap[-1,1]\big|\lesssim\min\{2^{-j_{i}-l_{i}},1\}=2^{-(j_{i}+l_{i})_{+}}\,.

In case
l_{i}\geq 0
or if
j_{i}=0
this directly implies (
51
), since then
(j_{i}+l_{i})_{+}=j_{i}+l_{i}^{+}
.
It remains the case, where both
l_{i}<0
and
j_{i}>0
.
Assuming
j_{i}>0
, the only contributions to the support of
\text{I}_{j_{i},k_{i},l_{i}}
come from the kink points
k^{(m)}_{j_{i}+l_{i},k_{i}}
of
\psi_{j_{i}+l_{i},k_{i}}\circ\rho_{1}
.
If
\dist(x_{i},k^{(m)}_{j_{i}+l_{i},k_{i}})>2\cdot 2^{-j_{i}}
for all kink points
k^{(m)}_{j_{i}+l_{i},k_{i}}

then clearly

\displaystyle\text{I}_{j_{i},k_{i},l_{i}}(x_{i})=\int\limits_{-1}^{1}\big|\Delta^{2}_{h_{i}2^{-j_{i}}}(\psi_{j_{i}+l_{i},k_{i}}\circ\rho_{1},x_{i})\big|\,\mathrm{d}h_{i}=0\,.

(53)

Since
\psi_{j_{i}+l_{i},k_{i}}
has either
3
or
7
kink points (see (
32
)), there are further at most
28
kink points of
\psi_{j_{i}+l_{i},k_{i}}\circ\rho_{1}
in
[-2,2]
, which is the maximal range that might be relevant for the value of
\text{I}_{j_{i},k_{i},l_{i}}(x_{i})
if
x_{i}\in[-1,1]
and
j_{i}>0
. Hence, we obtain for
j_{i}>0

\displaystyle\big|\operatorname{supp}(\text{I}_{j_{i},k_{i},l_{i}})\cap[-1,1]\big|\lesssim 2^{-j_{i}}\,.

Since
j_{i}=j_{i}+l_{i}^{+}
if
l_{i}<0
, this settles the proof of (
51
).

The last estimate also
shows (
52
) for
j_{i}>0
. For the remaining case
j_{i}=0
of (
52
) simply note that

\displaystyle\text{I}^{[1]}_{0,k_{i},l_{i}}\equiv 0\,.\;\text{$\square$}

(54)

•

Next, we prove the upper bounds

\displaystyle\big\|\text{I}^{[0]}_{j_{i},k_{i},l_{i}}\big\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{l_{i}^{-}}\,,

(55)

\displaystyle\big\|\text{I}^{[1]}_{j_{i},k_{i},l_{i}}\big\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-|l_{i}|}\,.

(56)

Proof of (
55
) and (
56
).

We begin with the case
j_{i}=0
of (
55
) and estimate

\displaystyle\text{I}^{[0]}_{0,k_{i},l_{i}}(x_{i})\leq\int\limits_{-1}^{1}\big|\psi_{l_{i},k_{i}}\circ\rho_{1}(x_{i})\big|\,\mathrm{d}h_{i}\lesssim|\psi_{l_{i},k_{i}}\circ\rho_{1}(x_{i})|\lesssim\min\{2^{l_{i}},1\}=2^{l^{-}_{i}}\,,

where we use
\psi_{l_{i},k_{i}}=0
for
l_{i}<-1
and
|\psi_{l_{i},k_{i}}\circ\rho_{1}(x_{i})|\lesssim 1
. For proving (
55
) in case
j_{i}>0
, we record on the one hand

\displaystyle\text{I}_{j_{i},k_{i},l_{i}}(x_{i})=\int\limits_{-1}^{1}\big|\Delta^{2}_{h_{i}2^{-j_{i}}}(\psi_{j_{i}+l_{i},k_{i}}\circ\rho_{1},x_{i})\big|\,\mathrm{d}h_{i}\lesssim 1\,.

(57)

Hereby, the previous estimate (
57
) holds true since, according to (
41
),

\displaystyle|\Delta_{h_{i}2^{-j_{i}}}^{2}(\psi_{j_{i}+l_{i},k_{i}}\circ\rho_{1},x_{i})|\leq 4\|\psi_{j_{i}+l_{i},k_{i}}\circ\rho_{1}\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq 4\|\psi_{0,0}\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim 1\,.

(58)

On the other hand, we get from the Lipschitz properties of
\psi_{j_{i}+l_{i},k_{i}}
and
\rho_{1}
(see (
41
) and (
42
)) the estimate

\displaystyle|\Delta_{h_{i}2^{-j_{i}}}^{2}(\psi_{j_{i}+l_{i},k_{i}}\circ\rho_{1},x_{i})|\lesssim L(\psi_{j_{i}+l_{i},k_{i}}\circ\rho_{1})|h_{i}|2^{-j_{i}}\leq L(\psi_{j_{i}+l_{i},k_{i}})|h_{i}|2^{-j_{i}}=L(\psi_{0,0})|h_{i}|2^{l_{i}}\lesssim|h_{i}|2^{l_{i}}

and thus, if
j_{i}>0
,

\displaystyle\text{I}_{j_{i},k_{i},l_{i}}(x_{i})=\int\limits_{-1}^{1}\big|\Delta^{2}_{h_{i}2^{-j_{i}}}(\psi_{j_{i}+l_{i},k_{i}}\circ\rho_{1},x_{i})\big|\,\mathrm{d}h_{i}\lesssim 2^{l_{i}}\,.

(59)

Together with (
57
), the last estimate (
59
) in particular implies

\displaystyle\text{I}^{[0]}_{j_{i},k_{i},l_{i}}(x_{i})\lesssim\min\{2^{l_{i}},1\}=2^{l^{-}_{i}}\quad\text{for }j_{i}>0\,,

finishing the proof of (
55
).

At last, we turn to the proof of (
56
) and recall (
54
)
to see that (
56
) is trivially fulfilled in case
j_{i}=0
.
In order to prove (
56
) in case
j_{i}>0
we use (
58
) and the fact that, if
x_{i}\notin S_{j_{i},k_{i},l_{i}}=\operatorname{supp}\big(\psi_{j_{i}+l_{i},k_{i}}\circ\rho_{1}\big)
,

\displaystyle|\operatorname{supp}(h_{i}\mapsto\Delta^{2}_{h_{i}2^{-j_{i}}}(\psi_{j_{i}+l_{i},k_{i}}\circ\rho_{1},x_{i}))\cap[-1,1]|

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-l_{i}}

to deduce

\displaystyle\text{I}_{j_{i},k_{i},l_{i}}(x_{i})=\int\limits_{-1}^{1}\big|\Delta^{2}_{h_{i}2^{-j_{i}}}(\psi_{j_{i}+l_{i},k_{i}}\circ\rho_{1},x_{i})\big|\,\mathrm{d}h_{i}\lesssim 2^{-l_{i}}

in case
x_{i}\notin S_{j_{i},k_{i},l_{i}}
. Combined with estimate (
59
), estimate (
56
) is obtained.
\square

Altogether, our estimates (
51
), (
52
), (
55
), (
56
) show that the multivariate functions
\text{I}^{[\bar{\varepsilon}]}_{\bar{j},\bar{k},\bar{l}}
satisfy

\displaystyle\big|\operatorname{supp}(\text{I}^{[\bar{\varepsilon}]}_{\bar{j},\bar{k},\bar{l}})\cap[-1,1]^{d}\big|\lesssim 2^{-\bar{j}-\bar{l}_{+}}A(\bar{\varepsilon},\bar{l})\qquad\text{and}\qquad\big\|\text{I}^{[\bar{\varepsilon}]}_{\bar{j},\bar{k},\bar{l}}\big\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim 2^{\bar{l}_{-}}A(\bar{\varepsilon},\bar{l})^{-1}

(60)

if we define

\displaystyle A(\bar{\varepsilon},\bar{l}):=\prod_{i\in e(\bar{\varepsilon})}2^{l_{i}^{+}}\quad\text{with}\quad e(\bar{\varepsilon})=\{i:\varepsilon_{i}\neq 0\}\,.

Let us next count the number of local overlaps of the sum
\sum_{\bar{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\lambda_{\bar{j}+\bar{l},\bar{k}}|\text{I}^{[\bar{\varepsilon}]}_{\bar{j},\bar{k},\bar{l}}(x)
, i.e. its number of non-zero summands when
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
is fixed. Due to the
2
-periodicity of the functions
\text{I}^{[\bar{\varepsilon}]}_{\bar{j},\bar{k},\bar{l}}
it hereby suffices to consider
x\in[-1,1]^{d}
. Further, we only aim for an upper bound. It is therefore enough to consider the values

O^{[\bar{\varepsilon}]}_{\bar{j},\bar{l}}(x):=\big|\big\{\bar{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\penalty\ :\penalty\ \text{I}^{[\bar{\varepsilon}]}_{\bar{j},\bar{k},\bar{l}}(x)\neq 0\big\}\big|\,.

The supports of the functions
\text{I}^{[\bar{\varepsilon}]}_{\bar{j},\bar{k},\bar{l}}
will subsequently be denoted by

\displaystyle T^{[\bar{\varepsilon}]}_{\bar{j},\bar{k},\bar{l}}:=\operatorname{supp}\big(\text{I}^{[\bar{\varepsilon}]}_{\bar{j},\bar{k},\bar{l}}\big)=\operatorname{supp}\big(\text{I}^{[\varepsilon_{1}]}_{j_{1},k_{1},l_{1}}\big)\times\ldots\times\operatorname{supp}\big(\text{I}^{[\varepsilon_{d}]}_{j_{d},k_{d},l_{d}}\big)\,.

Moving in
i
-direction,
k_{i}\rightarrow k_{i}+1
, the wavelets

\psi_{j_{i}+l_{i},k_{i}}
, which are nontrivial only for
j_{i}+l_{i}\geq-1
, are shifted by a length of

2^{-(j_{i}+l_{i})_{+}}
. Further, only if their support intersects with
[0,1]

the functions
\text{I}^{[\varepsilon_{i}]}_{j_{i},k_{i},l_{i}}
do not vanish. Hence, if we let
T^{[\varepsilon_{i}]}_{j_{i},k_{i},l_{i}}:=\operatorname{supp}\big(\text{I}^{[\varepsilon_{i}]}_{j_{i},k_{i},l_{i}}\big)

for
i\in[d]
, we have
T^{[\varepsilon_{i}]}_{j_{i},k_{i},l_{i}}\neq\emptyset
only for
k_{i}
in the range

\displaystyle k_{i}\in\{-3,-1,0,\ldots,2^{(j_{i}+l_{i})_{+}}\}\,.

(61)

For this observation, it is also necessary to note that

\operatorname{supp}\big(\psi_{0,0}\big)=[0,3]
and
\operatorname{supp}\big(\psi_{-1,0}\big)=[0,2]
for the generating wavelets
\psi_{0,0}
and
\psi_{-1,0}
(see (
32
)).

The range (
61
) of relevant
k_{i}
gives an initial bound on the maximal number of overlaps. However, locally, we can bound more sharply due to the shrinking support of the wavelets at rising scales.
Let us first consider the case
\varepsilon_{i}=0
,
when
\text{T}^{[0]}_{j_{i},k_{i},l_{i}}\subset S_{j_{i},k_{i},l_{i}}
(see (
49
)) and
S_{j_{i},k_{i},l_{i}}\cap[-1,1]
is contained in at most
2
intervals of length

3\cdot 2^{-(j_{i}+l_{i})_{+}}
since
|\operatorname{supp}(\psi_{j_{i}+l_{i},k_{i}})|\leq 3\cdot 2^{-(j_{i}+l_{i})_{+}}
. Indeed, the fact that here the size of the support

\mathcal{O}(2^{-(j_{i}+l_{i})_{+}})
matches the size of the shifts of the wavelets results in just
\mathcal{O}(1)
local overlaps of the functions
\text{I}^{[0]}_{j_{i},k_{i},l_{i}}
,
k_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}
.
In case
\varepsilon_{i}=1
, we have
T^{[1]}_{0,k_{i},l_{i}}=\emptyset

according to (
54
) and hence no overlaps if
j_{i}=0
.
If
j_{i}>0
, we know from (
53
) that
the set
T^{[1]}_{j_{i},k_{i},l_{i}}\cap[-1,1]
is contained in at most
28
intervals of length
2^{1-j_{i}}

around the kink points
k^{(m)}_{j_{i}+l_{i},k_{i}}
of
\psi_{j_{i}+l_{i},k_{i}}\circ\rho_{1}
in
[-2,2]
.
As a result, relating the length of these intervals to the size
2^{-(j_{i}+l_{i})_{+}}
of the shifts, we obtain
in this case the upper bound
\mathcal{O}(2^{l_{i}^{+}})
for the number of overlaps of the functions
\text{I}^{[1]}_{j_{i},k_{i},l_{i}}
,
k_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}
.

Altogether, our analysis can be summarized in the estimate

\displaystyle O^{[\bar{\varepsilon}]}_{\bar{j},\bar{l}}(x)\lesssim A(\bar{\varepsilon},\bar{l})\,,

(62)

where the implicit constant is independent of all parameters
\bar{j}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}
,

\bar{l}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
,
\bar{\varepsilon}\in\{0,1\}^{d}
, and
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
Building upon the estimate (
62
), we obtain

\displaystyle\Big(\sum_{\bar{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\lambda_{\bar{j}+\bar{l},\bar{k}}|\text{I}^{[\bar{\varepsilon}]}_{\bar{j},\bar{k},\bar{l}}(x)\Big)^{p}\lesssim A(\bar{\varepsilon},\bar{l})^{(p-1)_{+}}\sum_{\bar{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\lambda_{\bar{j}+\bar{l},\bar{k}}|^{p}\Big(\text{I}^{[\bar{\varepsilon}]}_{\bar{j},\bar{k},\bar{l}}(x)\Big)^{p}\,,

which, in view of (
60
), yields

\displaystyle\Big\|\sum_{\bar{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\lambda_{\bar{j}+\bar{l},\bar{k}}|\text{I}^{[\bar{\varepsilon}]}_{\bar{j},\bar{k},\bar{l}}\Big\|_{L_{p}([-1,1]^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim A(\bar{\varepsilon},\bar{l})^{(1-1/p)_{+}}2^{l_{-}}A(\bar{\varepsilon},\bar{l})^{-1}\Big(\sum_{\bar{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\lambda_{\bar{j}+\bar{l},\bar{k}}|^{p}\|\chi_{T^{[\bar{\varepsilon}]}_{\bar{j},\bar{k},\bar{l}}}\|_{L_{1}([-1,1]^{d})}\Big)^{1/p}

\displaystyle\lesssim A(\bar{\varepsilon},\bar{l})^{(1-1/p)_{+}}2^{l_{-}}2^{(-\bar{j}-\bar{l}_{+})/p}A(\bar{\varepsilon},\bar{l})^{1/p-1}\Big(\sum_{\bar{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\lambda_{\bar{j}+\bar{l},\bar{k}}|^{p}\Big)^{1/p}\,.

Now note that
\sigma_{p}=(\tfrac{1}{p}-1)_{+}=(1-\tfrac{1}{p})_{+}+\tfrac{1}{p}-1
and put

\displaystyle B_{p}(\bar{l}):=\prod_{i\in[d]}B_{p}(l_{i})\quad\text{with}\quad B_{p}(l_{i}):=\begin{cases}2^{l_{i}}\quad&,\,l_{i}<0\,,\\
2^{-l_{i}/p}&,\,l_{i}\geq 0\,.\end{cases}

Then we can write

\displaystyle\Big\|\sum_{\bar{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\lambda_{\bar{j}+\bar{l},\bar{k}}|\text{I}^{[\bar{\varepsilon}]}_{\bar{j},\bar{k},\bar{l}}(x)\Big\|_{L_{p}([-1,1]^{d})}\lesssim A(\bar{\varepsilon},\bar{l})^{\sigma_{p}}B_{p}(\bar{l})2^{-\bar{j}/p}\Big(\sum_{\bar{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\lambda_{\bar{j}+\bar{l},\bar{k}}|^{p}\Big)^{1/p}\,.

Since
1\leq A(\bar{\varepsilon},\bar{l})\leq 2^{\bar{l}_{+}}
,
we finally arrive at

\displaystyle\Big\|\sum_{\bar{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\lambda_{\bar{j}+\bar{l},\bar{k}}|\text{I}^{[\bar{\varepsilon}]}_{\bar{j},\bar{k},\bar{l}}\Big\|_{L_{p}([-1,1]^{d})}\lesssim 2^{\sigma_{p}\bar{l}_{+}}B_{p}(\bar{l})2^{-\bar{j}/p}\Big(\sum_{\bar{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\lambda_{\bar{j}+\bar{l},\bar{k}}|^{p}\Big)^{1/p}\,,

where the right-hand side is independent of
\bar{\varepsilon}
. Altogether, we thus obtain

\displaystyle\Big\|\mathcal{R}^{e(\bar{j})}_{2}(f_{\bar{j}+\bar{l}}\circ\rho,2^{-\bar{j}},\cdot)\Big\|_{L_{p}([-1,1]^{d})}\lesssim 2^{\sigma_{p}\bar{l}_{+}}B_{p}(\bar{l})2^{-\bar{j}/p}\Big(\sum_{\bar{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\lambda_{\bar{j}+\bar{l},\bar{k}}|^{p}\Big)^{1/p}\,.

(63)

We plug this estimate into (
47
). This yields

\displaystyle\Big[\eqref{fff1}\Big]^{u}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\bar{l}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\Big(\sum_{\bar{j}\in\mathbb{N}^{d}_{0}}\Big(2^{\sigma_{p}\bar{l}_{+}}B_{p}(\bar{l})2^{-\bar{l}(r-1/p)}2^{|\bar{j}+\bar{l}|_{1}(r-1/p)}\Big(\sum_{\bar{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\lambda_{\bar{j}+\bar{l},\bar{k}}|^{p}\Big)^{1/p}\Big)^{q}\Big)^{u/q}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\bar{l}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\Big(B_{p}(\bar{l})2^{\sigma_{p}\bar{l}_{+}}2^{-\bar{l}(r-1/p)}\Big)^{u}\cdot\Big(\sum_{\bar{j}\in\mathbb{N}^{d}_{-1}}2^{|\bar{j}|_{1}(r-1/p)q}\Big(\sum_{\bar{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\lambda_{\bar{j},\bar{k}}\big|^{p}\Big)^{q/p}\Big)^{u/q}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big(\sum\limits_{\bar{j}\in\mathbb{N}_{-1}^{d}}2^{|\bar{j}|_{1}(r-1/p)q}\Big(\sum\limits_{\bar{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\lambda_{\bar{j},\bar{k}}|^{p}\Big)^{q/p}\Big)^{u/q}\,,

since for
\sigma_{p}<r<1+\tfrac{1}{p}
we get

\displaystyle\sum_{\bar{l}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\Big(B_{p}(\bar{l})2^{\sigma_{p}\bar{l}_{+}}2^{-\bar{l}(r-1/p)}\Big)^{u}=\sum_{\bar{l}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\Big(\prod_{i\in[d]}2^{l^{-}_{i}(1+1/p-r)}2^{-l^{+}_{i}(r-\sigma_{p})}\Big)^{u}<\infty\,.

Hence, we have
\|\mathcal{P}(f)\|_{S^{r}_{p,q}B(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\lesssim\|\{\lambda_{\bar{l},\bar{k}}\}_{\bar{l},\bar{k}}\|_{s^{r}_{p,q}b(\Gamma^{d})}
. Invoking Proposition
5.3
from Section
5
then shows (
44
) in case
r<2
. The proof of the first statement
of Theorem
4.3
is thus finished.

To prove the second statement, we distinguish between the cases
\tfrac{1}{3}<p<1

and
1<p\leq\infty
. Assuming
\tfrac{1}{3}<p<1
,
note that in this range
r=2
and
\sigma_{p}<2<1+\tfrac{1}{p}
.
Hence, we obtain as above

\|\mathcal{P}(f)\|_{S^{r}_{p,\infty}B(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\lesssim\|\{\lambda_{\bar{l},\bar{k}}\}_{\bar{l},\bar{k}}\|_{s^{r}_{p,\infty}b(\Gamma^{d})}
. Lemma
5.5
then yields the result in this case.
Assuming
1<p\leq\infty
, we need to show

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{P}(f)\|_{S^{r}_{p,\infty}B(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\lesssim\|f\|_{S^{r}_{p,1}B(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\quad\text{with $r=1+\tfrac{1}{p}$}\,.

Here, we argue as before until estimate (
46
). From there, we get

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{P}(f)\|_{S^{r}_{p,\infty}B(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\lesssim\sup_{\bar{j}\in\mathbb{N}^{d}_{0}}\sum_{\bar{l}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}2^{|\bar{j}|_{1}r}\Big\|\mathcal{R}^{e(\bar{j})}_{2}(f_{\bar{j}+\bar{l}}\circ\rho,2^{-\bar{j}},\cdot)\Big\|_{L_{p}([-1,1]^{d})}\,.

Plugging in (
63
) with
\sigma_{p}=0
and
r=1+\tfrac{1}{p}
, the right-hand side can be further bounded from above by

\displaystyle\sup_{\bar{j}\in\mathbb{N}^{d}_{0}}\sum_{\bar{l}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}B_{p}(\bar{l})2^{-\bar{l}}2^{|\bar{j}+\bar{l}|_{1}}\Big(\sum_{\bar{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\lambda_{\bar{j}+\bar{l},\bar{k}}|^{p}\Big)^{1/p}\lesssim\sup_{\bar{j}\in\mathbb{N}^{d}_{0}}\sum_{\bar{l}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}2^{|\bar{j}+\bar{l}|_{1}}\Big(\sum_{\bar{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\lambda_{\bar{j}+\bar{l},\bar{k}}|^{p}\Big)^{1/p}=\|\{\lambda_{\bar{l},\bar{k}}\}_{\bar{l},\bar{k}}\|_{s^{1+1/p}_{p,1}b(\Gamma^{d})}\,.

Note that
B_{p}(\bar{l})\leq B_{\infty}(\bar{l})=2^{\bar{l}_{-}}
and therefore
\max_{\bar{l}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}B_{p}(\bar{l})2^{-\bar{l}}=1
.
Taking Proposition
5.3
into account, the proof of this case is also finished.
∎

5
Chui-Wang characterization of
S^{r}_{p,q}B(\mathbb{R}^{d})

Let
\{\psi_{\bar{j},\bar{k}}\}_{\bar{j},\bar{k}}
and
\{\tilde{\psi}_{\bar{j},\bar{k}}\}_{\bar{j},\bar{k}}

be the primal and dual Chui-Wang wavelet systems from Subsection
4.1
.
In Theorem
5.1
below, we give
characterizations of
functions
f\in S^{r}_{p,q}B(\mathbb{R}^{d})

in terms of their Chui-Wang coefficients

\displaystyle\lambda_{\bar{j},\bar{k}}(f):=2^{\bar{j}_{+}}\langle f,\psi_{\bar{j},\bar{k}}\rangle_{\raisebox{-1.80835pt}{\hbox to3.98pt{\vbox to3.98pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\thinspace\lower-1.9919pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
